1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	This protocol uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
4	contains instructions on how a commit cycle is accomplished.
5	
6	The artifact a cycle produces is whatever the bot generates from
7	the conversation — code, prose, an image, a song, a screenplay.
8	The steps below use a Rust crate as the running example (the
9	cargo cycle, `Cargo.toml` versioning); substitute your medium's
10	equivalents — this project's manifest is recorded in
11	[versioning.md](versioning.md).
12	
13	## Cycles
14	
15	A cycle has three phases:
16	
17	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's
18	  first commit, when it needs setup (a lightweight cycle omits
19	  it and starts at `-1` — see
20	  [versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)). Sets up the
21	  cycle:
22	  - Bump the version-of-record to `X.Y.Z-0` (where it lives
23	    and the suffix scheme are project-specific — see
24	    [versioning.md](versioning.md)).
25	  - Pick up a `## Todo` item (typically the top-ranked,
26	    #1) into `## In Progress` (bold title + succinct problem
27	    statement + plan ladder).
28	  - Open the [chores section](#chores-sections).
29	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the
30	  commits that implement the change. As many as the change
31	  needs; each runs through the
32	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
33	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's
34	  last commit. Bookkeeping only:
35	  - Move the picked-up item to a one-line entry in
36	    `## Done`.
37	  - Move the `## In Progress` block into the
38	    [chores section](#chores-sections).
39	  - Optionally update `notes/README.md` if functionality
40	    changed.
41	
42	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
43	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
44	result must always be published at close-out. See
45	[Pushing](#pushing).
46	
47	**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
48	warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
49	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
50	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
51	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
52	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
53	for the local-ladder mechanics.
54	
55	## Chores sections
56	
57	A **chores section** is a `##` section in
58	`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording landed work. In
59	general, every commit that lands on the permanent branch
60	should have a reference to it on a `Commits:` list in a
61	chores file.
62	
63	The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
64	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
65	with the title it records (e.g. `## refactor: foo bar`),
66	followed by an **empty `Commits:`** line. The narrative
67	body stays empty until close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out));
68	the `Commits:` line is backfilled later, once the commit is
69	permanent (see [Commits backfill](#commits-backfill) below).
70	
71	Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
72	design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live in
73	AGENTS.md [Chores conventions](../AGENTS.md#chores-conventions).
74	
75	### Commits backfill
76	
77	A chores section's `Commits:` line cites the commit(s) it
78	records, by SHA — but a SHA isn't stable until the commit
79	lands on a **permanent branch** (`main`, or a long-lived
80	release/patch branch that won't be rewritten); a rebase or
81	squash rewrites it on the way. So:
82	
83	- A chores section is **opened with an empty `Commits:`** line.
84	- **Backfill once the commit is on a permanent branch**,
85	  where its SHA is final. A commit can't record its own SHA
86	  (that would change the hash), so the fill always lands one
87	  push later: **each push backfills the `Commits:` of the
88	  commits the previous push made permanent.** On a topic
89	  branch the sections instead wait until the branch lands —
90	  so no SHA is ever written that a later rebase could
91	  invalidate.
92	
93	Use `[[N]]` refs — several as `[[N]],[[M]]` only when one
94	section records multiple commits (a merge non-ff close-out) —
95	with the commit URL + 40-hex SHA in the file's `# References`
96	(format in AGENTS.md
97	[Chores commit references](../AGENTS.md#chores-commit-references)).
98	A section's `##` title matches its commit title, so a rare
99	deliberate rewrite of a permanent-branch commit re-syncs via
100	`git log --grep "<title>"`.
101	
102	The per-push cadence is a project choice, not dogma — a
103	**per-close-out** model (recording a cycle's SHAs at its
104	close-out) is equally valid. The one invariant: a recorded
105	SHA must be permanent.
106	
107	## Preparation
108	
109	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`), when the cycle needs
110	setup (a lightweight cycle omits it — see
111	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)):
112	
113	- **Bump the version-of-record** to `X.Y.Z-0`. Where it
114	  lives, the suffix scheme, and any derived files (a
115	  lockfile, a sourced manifest version) are
116	  project-specific — see [versioning.md](versioning.md).
117	- **Move a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one) into
118	  `## In Progress` and the todo item should have:
119	  - A **bold title line** — that will be the chores
120	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
121	  - A **succinct problem statement**; add if one is needed
122	  - A **plan ladder**.
123	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
124	  append a `##` header with the title it records — the
125	  cycle's anticipated close-out title.
126	
127	## Work-N
128	
129	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
130	implement the change. As many as needed:
131	
132	- Each commit runs through the
133	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
134	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
135	  visibility).
136	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
137	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
138	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
139	  scope grows enough (see
140	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
141	
142	## Close-out
143	
144	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
145	only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
146	what happens post-squash:
147	
148	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
149	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
150	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
151	  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
152	  placeholder opened during Preparation):
153	  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
154	    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
155	    chores `##` header already carries that string).
156	  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
157	  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
158	    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
159	    back-reference.
160	  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
161	    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
162	  - Replace `## In Progress` with
163	    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
164	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
165	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
166	
167	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
168	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
169	see [Pushing](#pushing).
170	
171	## Numbering
172	
173	Each commit's phase is encoded in the version suffix — `-0`
174	Preparation, `-1`/`-2`/… Work, bare `X.Y.Z` Close-out,
175	recursively for sub-cycles. The full scheme — disambiguation,
176	nesting, optional Preparation, the project's version-of-record
177	format, and the per-phase bump — lives in
178	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering), which is the
179	single source of truth for this repo's versioning.
180	
181	## Per-commit flow
182	
183	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
184	through:
185	
186	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
187	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
188	2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
189	   for the loop-and-squash technique).
190	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
191	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit.
192	4. **Validate the artifact** — a medium-specific step, skip-able
193	   for notes-only commits, mandatory at close-out. For the Rust
194	   example the cargo cycle is:
195	   1. `cargo fmt`
196	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
197	   3. `cargo test`
198	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
199	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
200	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
201	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
202	   changes and we iterate until complete.
203	6. **Write the commit description** — see
204	   [Commit description](#commit-description).
205	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
206	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
207	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
208	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
209	   keeps the verb visible):
210	
211	   ```
212	   jj commit -m \
213	   "<type>: <short description>" \
214	   -m "<intro paragraph>
215	
216	   - file1: gist
217	   - file2: gist
218	
219	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
220	   -R .
221	   ```
222	
223	**Two overrides apply:**
224	
225	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
226	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
227	  surface it; don't push through.
228	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
229	  a review or question forward.
230	
231	## Commit description
232	
233	[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/):
234	
235	```
236	<type>: <short description>
237	```
238	
239	Titles carry **no trailing `(<version>)` suffix**. The
240	version-of-record — where it lives and its bump cadence, see
241	[versioning.md](versioning.md) — is useful for confirming
242	you're running the version you're testing, not a per-commit
243	title marker. We think per-commit version-in-title is
244	unstable on large projects: many changes are in flight
245	simultaneously, and a
246	version is only stable once merged into the main repo — so
247	titles don't carry one.
248	
249	### Title
250	
251	- ≤50 chars total.
252	- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
253	  `docs`, `chore`.
254	- Favor terse phrasings.
255	- **Distinct per step** — each of a cycle's commits gets its
256	  own descriptive title (no shared cycle title with a step
257	  marker). Share a greppable stem across the cycle's titles
258	  (e.g. `ring buffer`) so `git log --grep` collects them; the
259	  chores section header matches the close-out title. See
260	  AGENTS.md
261	  [Conventional-commit shape](../AGENTS.md#conventional-commit-shape-ladder--chores--commit).
262	
263	### Body
264	
265	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
266	wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:
267	
268	- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
269	  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
270	  files with multiple distinct changes:
271	
272	  ```
273	  - path/to/file1
274	    - first distinct change
275	    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
276	       with continuation indented 5
277	  ```
278	
279	  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
280	  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
281	  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
282	  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
283	
284	- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
285	  in-session activity rather than code changes.
286	
287	### Trailer
288	
289	`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
290	[Cross-repo linking (ochid trailers)](../AGENTS.md#cross-repo-linking-ochid-trailers)
291	in AGENTS.md for the convention.
292	
293	For breaking changes, use the hyphenated `BREAKING-CHANGE:`
294	trailer key. `BREAKING CHANGE:` (with a space) is the only
295	space-separated key the Conventional Commits spec allows; the
296	hyphenated form is also valid and avoids the space ambiguity.
297	
298	## Reviewing changes
299	
300	Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
301	on the way to commit. The user opens diffs in their
302	editor (Zed, VSCode); jj commands are for terminal:
303	
304	- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
305	- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
306	- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
307	- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev
308	
309	Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
310	it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
311	`jj show -r @-`.
312	
313	See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
314	close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
315	are in [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets).
316	
317	## Pushing
318	
319	### Policy
320	
321	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
322	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
323	the cycle's result must be published.
324	
325	**Approval is per-push.** Every push — any repo, any kind:
326	cycle push, interim backup, recovery/surgery force-push —
327	happens only after the user has reviewed the changes to be
328	published and explicitly approved that specific push.
329	Approval of a plan that *includes* a push does not authorize
330	the push itself; stop and ask again at the moment of pushing.
331	
332	**Default is interactive; an explicit scoped delegation waives
333	the gates.** The gates above — per-push approval, the
334	commit-description review (show title+body and stop), and the
335	hard stop after push/finalize — are the *interactive default*.
336	They yield when the user **explicitly** delegates a complete,
337	bounded task and authorizes carrying it through ("do all of X
338	and push each step, don't check in"). The bot then proceeds
339	through that task's commits and pushes without stopping, and
340	continues past each push to the next step. Conditions:
341	
342	- **Explicit grant** — never inferred from a task merely being
343	  well-scoped; the user's words must authorize unattended
344	  completion. "Commit and push" (or "then push") names the
345	  destination, not a waiver: it authorizes the push *after*
346	  the normal work review and description review, not skipping
347	  them. Only wording that explicitly waives the stops ("don't
348	  check in", "no need to review", "carry it through
349	  unattended") waives them.
350	- **Bounded goal** — covers the named task only; does not carry
351	  to the next task or a vaguer follow-on.
352	- **Destructive ops still pause** — delegation covers the task's
353	  ordinary commits and pushes; it does *not* pre-authorize a
354	  genuinely irreversible action (force-push over published
355	  history, history rewrite, deleting a remote branch). Those can
356	  permanently destroy work and aren't a normal cycle step, so the
357	  bot flags one before acting. An ordinary delegated cycle never
358	  reaches this.
359	- **Still transparent** — report each commit/push as it lands
360	  (title + outcome) so the user can catch up.
361	- **When in doubt, ask** — ambiguous authorization falls back to
362	  per-push approval.
363	
364	### Shape at close-out push
365	
366	At close-out the cycle's *work* is done; its *published
367	shape* is the remaining choice, made at push time. Surface
368	the options and get user approval before pushing — once on
369	the target, changing shape is a remote rewrite (force-push,
370	needs approval), so choose deliberately.
371	
372	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
373	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
374	  focused on one or two files.
375	- **Merge non-ff** *(current default)* — `main` gains a
376	  merge commit (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via
377	  two parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
378	  shape. See [Merge non-ff recipe](#merge-non-ff-recipe)
379	  for the full setup sequence.
380	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
381	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
382	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
383	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
384	
385	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
386	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
387	
388	### Merge non-ff recipe
389	
390	Setting up a [Merge non-ff](#shape-at-close-out-push)
391	close-out is a fixed sequence. `<closeout>` is the cycle's
392	close-out commit, `<prev>` the previous cycle's close-out
393	(the current `main` tip), `<work-tip>` the cycle's last
394	Work commit:
395	
396	1. **Rebase the close-out into a merge** — `jj rebase -r
397	   <closeout> --onto <prev> --onto <work-tip>`.
398	   - `-r <closeout>` keeps the `<closeout>` commit in place.
399	   - `--onto <prev>` becomes its first parent (trunk).
400	   - `--onto <work-tip>` becomes the second parent.
401	
402	   Together these make `<closeout>` a merge of
403	   `<prev>` + `<work-tip>`, forming a trapezoidal commit.
404	2. **Use `jj new <merge>`** to add an empty `@` above the
405	   merge. The rebase left `@` *on* the now-content-bearing
406	   merge, which git/IDE diff views show as uncommitted;
407	   `jj new` restores the clean empty `@` on top.
408	3. **Push** — `jj git push --bookmark main -R .`.
409	
410	**Post-hoc caveat.** If the cycle was already pushed
411	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) its commits are
412	immutable: the rebase needs `--ignore-immutable` and the
413	push force-updates `main`.
414	The standard sequence assumes the merge is set up *before*
415	the close-out push.
416	
417	### vc-x1 push wrapper
418	
419	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
420	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
421	
422	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
423	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
424	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
425	planned:
426	
427	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
428	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
429	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
430	
431	### .claude cadence
432	
433	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
434	with every code commit in that push.
435	
436	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
437	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
438	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
439	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
440	
441	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
442	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
443	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
444	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
445	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
446	
447	Ending a session: if the user runs `/exit` there will be
448	session information created, which we don't worry about.
449	The user can close the terminal instead and `@` will
450	remain empty.
451	
452	### Bot communication at the reviews
453	
454	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
455	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
456	
457	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
458	  "Work complete. Please review."
459	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
460	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
461	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
462	  --body ...` invocation by default.
463	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
464	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
465	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
466	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
467	
468	### After push or finalize: stop and wait
469	
470	After you **push** (cross the remote boundary) or launch
471	**finalize** on the `.claude` repo (`vc-x1 finalize --repo
472	.claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach`) — by
473	hand or via the `vc-x1 push` wrapper — you **MUST NEVER**
474	proceed (next step, edit, tool call, text output) until the
475	user explicitly directs you to continue. **Even when the next
476	step seems obvious — wait.** Treat push-or-finalize as a hard
477	stop for the whole turn.
478	
479	`vc-x1 push` performs both as its tail stages — `push-app`
480	then the detached `vc-x1 finalize` on `.claude` — so there is
481	no gap left to speak in once the push is invoked. Put **all**
482	closing words *before* invoking it. The harness rejects an empty
483	turn, so it may force a visible token after the tool returns; if
484	so, emit a bare acknowledgment only (e.g. "landed") — never a
485	summary, verification, or next-step offer.
486	
487	Why (`.claude` is the live journal finalize snapshots →
488	squashes → pushes):
489	
490	- **`--detach`**: lets the tool return immediately so the
491	  harness can keep flushing the *trailing* session-data — the
492	  tool call's own transcript and any words emitted *before*
493	  the invoke — into the snapshot.
494	- **`--delay 10`**: gives the harness time to complete that
495	  flush before the snapshot is taken. It is **not** time for
496	  the bot to do more work.
497	- **silence**: the bot must not summarize or perform any work
498	  after the tool returns. The harness may force a visible token
499	  (it rejects an empty turn) — keep it to a bare acknowledgment,
500	  never a summary or a next step.
501	
502	**Known slip**: the bot has emitted a summary after launching
503	finalize. There is no "harmless" closing line — if it's worth
504	saying, say it *before* the invoke.
505	
506	### Recovery
507	
508	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
509	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
510	  finalize by hand:
511	
512	  ```
513	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
514	  ```
515	
516	- **Run finalize again if `@` is non-empty** after the
517	  finalize's squash-and-commit (also desirable after
518	  extra activity by the bot's agents).
519	  - Why: finalize snapshots `.claude` at `--delay`, but the
520	    bot keeps writing session data while it runs — the tool's
521	    own invocation plus the bot's closing response land
522	    *after* the snapshot.
523	  - Safe to repeat: bot session data is append-only, so a
524	    re-run never conflicts or overwrites. (This could
525	    change; it is not under the user's control.)
526	  - Fix: run `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push
527	    <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach` another time to capture
528	    that tail; one extra pass is generally enough.
529	  - No guarantees: events outside the bot's control can leave
530	    `@` non-empty — e.g. the bot's back end may decide to
531	    squash/consolidate session data, which can take minutes
532	    and land after the snapshot. The remedy is the same: just
533	    run finalize again. This is why a single pass is never
534	    guaranteed to leave `@` empty.
535	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
536	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
537	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
538	  stale stage.
539	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
540	  (e.g. updating AGENTS.md or memory) requires `jj
541	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
542	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
543	  push.
544	
545	## Iterative work
546	
547	When work for a single commit (the **target**) benefits
548	from incremental review, loop:
549	
550	1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target.
551	2. Make the next round of changes.
552	3. User reviews the round (see
553	   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).
554	4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a
555	   new empty `@`.
556	5. If not done, go to step 2.
557	
558	Same jj mechanics as a
559	[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
560	single-commit scope — the version
561	doesn't change.
562	
563	## Sub-cycle ladders
564	
565	When a Work commit subdivides into a sub-cycle (see
566	[Numbering](#numbering) for suffix nesting), its Work
567	commits typically live as a local jj `@` chain and
568	**collapse into the sub-cycle's Close-out** before the
569	parent cycle continues. Ladder commits are scratch —
570	for review and bisection only.
571	
572	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
573	
574	For each Work commit in the ladder:
575	
576	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
577	2. Do the commit's work.
578	3. Run the fast validation (Rust example: `cargo test
579	   --bins`). **Non-negotiable** — for code, build and clippy
580	   alone miss regressions until a later commit runs the full
581	   suite, raising bisection cost.
582	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — working title
583	   only; the sub-cycle Close-out collects everything
584	   into one final commit.
585	
586	### Navigating the ladder
587	
588	Common moves:
589	
590	- `jj log -r '<base>::' -R .` — see the whole ladder
591	  from its base.
592	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any ladder
593	  commit by chid prefix; useful for bisection.
594	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
595	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit in
596	  isolation.
597	
598	Modifications to any ladder commit rewrite it in place;
599	descendants auto-rebase.
600	
601	### Close-out: squash the ladder
602	
603	When all ladder Work commits are done and tests pass:
604	
605	```
606	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
607	```
608	
609	`<base>` is the parent of the first ladder commit; `-u`
610	keeps `@`'s description and discards the sources'.
611	After squash, history is linear: `<base> → @`;
612	intermediate commits are auto-abandoned.
613	
614	Then `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` as for any other commit.
615	
616	For N = 1 the squash is a no-op (`<base>..@-` is empty
617	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.
618	
619	### Recovery
620	
621	If a ladder commit goes wrong, back out without losing
622	prior commits:
623	
624	- **Discard the current commit.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
625	  drops it; you get a fresh empty `@` on the same
626	  parent.
627	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
628	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
629	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
630	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows
631	  the op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts
632	  to that point. Full undo — removes *all* ladder work
633	  after the chosen op. Use only to start over.
634	
635	# References
636	
637	- [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets) — revset primitives
638	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
639	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
640	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
641	  for squash-recipe experiments.
642	- The per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate exists because a
643	  regression introduced in an early ladder commit can go
644	  uncaught until a later commit runs the full suite, raising
645	  bisection cost.
646	
```

```
vc-x1 0.67.0

Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.

Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a
single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a
state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can
resume without re-doing completed stages.

Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)
→ message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →
commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both
→ push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /
commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via
`jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.
After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes
to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body
across resumes so only the first invocation needs them.

Usage: vc-x1 push [OPTIONS] [BOOKMARK]

Arguments:
  [BOOKMARK]
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
          
          Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
          without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an alias
          for scripts and for composition with other args. The two forms
          conflict if both supplied.

Options:
      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional)

  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
          
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

      --restart
          Clear any saved state file and start from stage 1

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --from <STAGE>
          Explicit stage to jump to (advanced / debug use)

          Possible values:
          - preflight:       Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest
          - review:          Present diff for the first approval gate
          - message:         Compose / edit the commit message; present for
            second gate
          - commit-app:      Commit the app repo
          - commit-claude:   Commit the `.claude` session repo (skipped if
            empty)
          - bookmark-both:   Advance both bookmarks to `@-`
          - push-app:        `jj git push --bookmark <b> -R .`
          - finalize-claude: `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <b>
            ...`

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --step
          Pause between every stage for an interactive approval gate

      --status
          Print where the saved state thinks we are and exit

      --recheck
          Re-run `preflight` even on resume (default: skip if last run
          succeeded)

      --no-finalize
          Stop before `finalize-claude` so it can be run manually

      --dry-run
          Print the exact commands for every stage without side effects

      --title <STR>
          Commit title (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

      --body <STR>
          Commit body (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

  -y, --yes
          Auto-approve interactive prompts (review gate, message-edit
          confirmation). Required when `--title` / `--body` aren't both supplied
          in non-interactive contexts

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
```

1	//! Zero-copy no_std SPSC ring buffer over a caller-provided
2	//! memory region, per [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]:
3	//!
4	//! - One `#[repr(C)]` [`Header`] spanning four cache lines
5	//!   (immutable geometry, producer index, consumer index, app
6	//!   user words), followed by M slots of N bytes.
7	//! - Indices are free-running `AtomicU32`s, masked only at slot
8	//!   access; full is `p - c == M`, no sacrificial slot.
9	//! - Messages move in place: the producer writes through a
10	//!   [`WriteSlot`] `&mut T`, the consumer reads through a
11	//!   [`ReadSlot`] `&T` — zerocopy traits bound `T`, no
12	//!   serialization step. Each side reserves at most one slot at
13	//!   a time; the guard holds the endpoint borrow until commit /
14	//!   release (or drop).
15	//!
16	//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]: https://github.com/winksaville/zc-ring-x1/blob/main/notes/ring-buffer-design.md
17	
18	#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]
19	
20	use core::marker::PhantomData;
21	use core::mem::{align_of, size_of};
22	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
23	
24	mod consumer;
25	pub mod policy;
26	mod pool;
27	mod producer;
28	mod registry;
29	
30	pub use consumer::{Consumer, Empty, ReadSlot};
31	pub use pool::{BufSlot, Exhausted, Pool, PoolHeader, PoolResolver};
32	pub use producer::{Full, Producer, WriteSlot};
33	pub use registry::{Desc, PoolId, PoolRegistry, RegistryError};
34	
35	/// Cache-line size the layout is built around.
36	///
37	/// - Slot size must be a multiple of this; slots and the region
38	///   itself must be aligned to it.
39	pub const CACHE_LINE_SIZE: usize = 64;
40	
41	/// Layout marker written by [`Ring::init`]; rejects foreign
42	/// regions on attach.
43	const MAGIC: u32 = 0x5A43_5231; // "ZCR1"
44	
45	/// Bumped on any change to the region layout.
46	const LAYOUT_VERSION: u32 = 2;
47	
48	/// Number of `AtomicU32` words in the header's app-owned
49	/// `user` line.
50	pub const USER_WORDS: usize = 16;
51	
52	/// Cache-line-aligned wrapper granting its field sole
53	/// ownership of the line.
54	///
55	/// - `repr(align(N))` accepts only an integer literal — it
56	///   cannot name [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] — so the `64` is written out
57	///   and a const assert ties them back together.
58	#[repr(C, align(64))]
59	struct CacheAligned<T>(T);
60	
61	const _: () = assert!(align_of::<CacheAligned<AtomicU32>>() == CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
62	
63	impl<T> core::ops::Deref for CacheAligned<T> {
64	    type Target = T;
65	    /// Access the wrapped value.
66	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
67	        &self.0
68	    }
69	}
70	
71	/// Control block at offset 0 of the region — one struct spanning
72	/// four cache lines so each concurrently-written index owns its
73	/// own line.
74	///
75	/// - line 0: geometry, written by [`Ring::init`] with `magic`
76	///   last (`Release`), read-only thereafter. Cold: per-op
77	///   paths use the handles' snapshots.
78	/// - line 1: `producer_idx`, written only by the producer.
79	/// - line 2: `consumer_idx`, written only by the consumer.
80	/// - line 3: `user`, app-owned scratch — last, so an app
81	///   overrun walks into its own slot data, not an index line.
82	/// - Every field is atomic: the region may be mapped by a peer
83	///   at any time, so even "immutable" fields must be free of
84	///   data races. `AtomicU32` has the same layout as `u32`, so
85	///   this does not affect `layout_version`.
86	#[repr(C)]
87	pub struct Header {
88	    /// Layout marker ([`MAGIC`]); stored last by init
89	    /// (`Release`), loaded first by attach (`Acquire`), so a
90	    /// peer that observes it also observes the geometry.
91	    magic: AtomicU32,
92	    /// Layout version ([`LAYOUT_VERSION`]).
93	    layout_version: AtomicU32,
94	    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] multiple.
95	    slot_size: AtomicU32,
96	    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^31`.
97	    capacity: AtomicU32,
98	    /// [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] this region was built with. The line size
99	    /// is a layout parameter (padding, offsets, slot granule),
100	    /// so builds must agree; attach validates it like the rest
101	    /// of the geometry.
102	    cache_line_size: AtomicU32,
103	    /// Free-running count of messages committed.
104	    producer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
105	    /// Free-running count of messages released.
106	    consumer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
107	    /// App-owned scratch line ([`USER_WORDS`] words): zeroed by
108	    /// init, then never read, written, or interpreted by the
109	    /// crate. See the design doc's "Blocking and user words".
110	    user: CacheAligned<[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS]>,
111	}
112	
113	const _: () = assert!(size_of::<Header>() == 4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
114	
115	/// Errors from region validation — [`Ring::init`] /
116	/// [`Ring::attach`] and [`Pool::init`] / [`Pool::attach`].
117	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
118	pub enum Error {
119	    /// Region is not [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned.
120	    Misaligned,
121	    /// Region is smaller than header + slots/buffers.
122	    TooSmall,
123	    /// Slot size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] multiple.
124	    BadSlotSize,
125	    /// Capacity is zero, not a power of two, or `> 2^31`.
126	    BadCapacity,
127	    /// Pool: buffer size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]
128	    /// multiple.
129	    BadBufSize,
130	    /// Pool: buffer count is zero or `u32::MAX` (the
131	    /// free-stack NIL sentinel).
132	    BadBufCount,
133	    /// Attach: magic mismatch — not a region of the expected
134	    /// kind.
135	    BadMagic,
136	    /// Attach: layout version mismatch.
137	    BadLayoutVersion,
138	    /// Attach: region built with a different [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`].
139	    BadCacheLine,
140	}
141	
142	/// A validated view over a ring region; split into the two
143	/// endpoint handles with [`Ring::split`].
144	///
145	/// - Geometry is snapshotted out of the header at
146	///   init/attach: per-op paths never re-read fields a peer
147	///   could scribble on, so a hostile peer can degrade the ring
148	///   to garbage messages or spurious Full/Empty, never to UB.
149	pub struct Ring<'a> {
150	    /// The region's control block.
151	    header: &'a Header,
152	    /// Base of the slot array (offset `size_of::<Header>()`).
153	    slots: *mut u8,
154	    /// Snapshot of `header.slot_size`.
155	    slot_size: u32,
156	    /// Snapshot of `header.capacity`.
157	    capacity: u32,
158	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
159	    mask: u32,
160	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
161	}
162	
163	impl<'a> Ring<'a> {
164	    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the ring over it.
165	    ///
166	    /// - `slot_size` — N bytes per slot, a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]
167	    ///   multiple.
168	    /// - `capacity` — M slots, a power of two `<= 2^31`.
169	    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned and at least
170	    ///   `size_of::<Header>() + M * N` bytes.
171	    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {
172	        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
173	        let len = region.len();
174	        // Taken exactly once: a second `as_mut_ptr()` would
175	        // retag the slice and invalidate `header` under Stacked
176	        // Borrows (Miri-verified).
177	        let base = region.as_mut_ptr();
178	        let header = header_ptr(base, len)?;
179	        if (len as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
180	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
181	        }
182	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the Header checked by
183	        // header_ptr; region is exclusively borrowed for 'a;
184	        // any byte pattern is a valid Header (all-atomic
185	        // fields, plain-byte padding).
186	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
187	        header
188	            .layout_version
189	            .store(LAYOUT_VERSION, Ordering::Relaxed);
190	        header.slot_size.store(slot_size, Ordering::Relaxed);
191	        header.capacity.store(capacity, Ordering::Relaxed);
192	        header
193	            .cache_line_size
194	            .store(CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, Ordering::Relaxed);
195	        header.producer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
196	        header.consumer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
197	        for word in header.user.iter() {
198	            word.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
199	        }
200	        // Published last: a peer that pre-mapped the region must
201	        // never observe MAGIC before the geometry it validates.
202	        // (Re-initializing a region a peer is already attached
203	        // to is outside the contract.)
204	        header.magic.store(MAGIC, Ordering::Release);
205	        // SAFETY: in bounds — region.len() >= header + slots.
206	        let slots = unsafe { base.add(size_of::<Header>()) };
207	        Ok(Ring {
208	            header,
209	            slots,
210	            slot_size,
211	            capacity,
212	            mask: capacity - 1,
213	            _region: PhantomData,
214	        })
215	    }
216	
217	    /// Attach to a region another process (or an earlier call)
218	    /// already initialized, validating its header.
219	    ///
220	    /// # Safety
221	    ///
222	    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive
223	    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a
224	    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).
225	    /// - No other producer attaches if this side will produce;
226	    ///   likewise for the consumer side (SPSC contract).
227	    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {
228	        let header = header_ptr(region, len)?;
229	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the Header checked by
230	        // header_ptr; caller guarantees the memory is live and
231	        // shared.
232	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
233	        // Acquire pairs with init's Release store of magic: a
234	        // valid magic means the geometry stores are visible.
235	        if header.magic.load(Ordering::Acquire) != MAGIC {
236	            return Err(Error::BadMagic);
237	        }
238	        if header.layout_version.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != LAYOUT_VERSION {
239	            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);
240	        }
241	        if header.cache_line_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32 {
242	            return Err(Error::BadCacheLine);
243	        }
244	        // Snapshot geometry once; per-op paths never re-read it.
245	        let slot_size = header.slot_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
246	        let capacity = header.capacity.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
247	        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
248	        if (len as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
249	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
250	        }
251	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + slots.
252	        let slots = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<Header>()) };
253	        Ok(Ring {
254	            header,
255	            slots,
256	            slot_size,
257	            capacity,
258	            mask: capacity - 1,
259	            _region: PhantomData,
260	        })
261	    }
262	
263	    /// Split into the producer and consumer endpoint handles.
264	    ///
265	    /// - Consuming `self` makes each handle exist at most once
266	    ///   per ring per process; cross-process, one producing and
267	    ///   one consuming process is the SPSC contract.
268	    pub fn split(self) -> (Producer<'a>, Consumer<'a>) {
269	        (
270	            Producer::new(
271	                self.header,
272	                self.slots,
273	                self.slot_size,
274	                self.capacity,
275	                self.mask,
276	            ),
277	            Consumer::new(self.header, self.slots, self.slot_size, self.mask),
278	        )
279	    }
280	}
281	
282	/// Validate a region base pointer and cast it to the `Header`
283	/// it must start with; shared by [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].
284	///
285	/// - Checks alignment and room for the `Header` itself. The
286	///   full-geometry length check stays with the caller: init
287	///   knows the geometry from its parameters, attach only after
288	///   reading it from the returned header.
289	/// - Returns a raw pointer — no deref here. Each caller
290	///   discharges its own safety obligation (init: exclusive
291	///   borrow; attach: the caller's shared-mapping contract).
292	fn header_ptr(base: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<*const Header, Error> {
293	    if !(base as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {
294	        return Err(Error::Misaligned);
295	    }
296	    if len < size_of::<Header>() {
297	        return Err(Error::TooSmall);
298	    }
299	    Ok(base as *const Header)
300	}
301	
302	/// Bytes needed for a region with the given geometry.
303	///
304	/// - Computed in u64: the usize product can wrap on 32-bit
305	///   targets (e.g. N = 2^26, M = 2^6), which would validate a
306	///   tiny region and let safe code write out of bounds.
307	fn region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> u64 {
308	    size_of::<Header>() as u64 + slot_size as u64 * capacity as u64
309	}
310	
311	/// Shared geometry checks for [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].
312	fn validate_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {
313	    if slot_size == 0 || !(slot_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {
314	        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);
315	    }
316	    // The `> 1 << 31` arm is unreachable (u32 powers of two are
317	    // <= 2^31); it stays to document the M bound the occupancy
318	    // math relies on.
319	    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > 1 << 31 {
320	        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);
321	    }
322	    Ok(())
323	}
324	
325	/// Check `T` fits a slot; called once per `reserve_slot` (both
326	/// endpoints).
327	///
328	/// - Panics on a type-geometry mismatch — that is a programming
329	///   error, not a runtime condition.
330	fn check_type<T>(slot_size: u32) {
```

1	//! Generic bench driver: the [`Bench`] trait, adaptive outer/inner
2	//! loop sizing, and the band-histogram report.
3	
4	use std::hint::black_box;
5	
6	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
7	
8	use crate::overhead::Overhead;
9	
10	const WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;
11	const ESTIMATE_STEPS: u64 = 1_000;
12	const ESTIMATE_SAMPLES: usize = 5;
13	const FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO: f64 = 10.0;
14	const MAX_INNER: u64 = 1_000;
15	
16	/// A benchmark workload: a named operation that `step()` performs
17	/// in a tight loop for sub-µs latency measurement. Implementors own
18	/// any setup state (channels, spawned threads, counters). `step()`
19	/// returns a value so the caller can `black_box` it against dead-code
20	/// elimination.
21	pub trait Bench {
22	    /// Human-readable name used in the report header.
23	    fn name(&self) -> &str;
24	
25	    /// Run one unit of work. Return any value derived from the work
26	    /// to defeat DCE — the caller black-boxes it.
27	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64;
28	}
29	
30	/// Runtime configuration for one [`run_adaptive`] call.
31	#[derive(Debug)]
32	pub struct RunCfg<'a> {
33	    /// Calibrated apparatus overhead (framing + loop/iter) used to
34	    /// compute the `adjusted` mean columns in the report.
35	    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,
36	    /// Wall-clock seconds budget for time-based runs. Ignored when
37	    /// `outer_override` is set.
38	    pub target_seconds: f64,
39	    /// Force a fixed outer-loop count, bypassing the time budget.
40	    pub outer_override: Option<u64>,
41	    /// Force a fixed inner-loop count, bypassing the
42	    /// overhead-dominated auto-sizing.
43	    pub inner_override: Option<u64>,
44	    /// Core pool for thread pinning. Indexed positionally with
45	    /// wrap-around via [`core_for`][RunCfg::core_for]; empty means
46	    /// no pinning.
47	    pub pin_cores: &'a [usize],
48	    /// When set, [`crate::tprobe::TProbe::report`] emits raw TSC
49	    /// ticks instead of nanoseconds. Plumbed from the `-t/--ticks`
50	    /// CLI flag.
51	    pub report_ticks: bool,
52	}
53	
54	impl RunCfg<'_> {
55	    /// CPU id for the bench's `thread_idx`-th thread, using
56	    /// wrap-around over the pool. Returns `None` when the pool is
57	    /// empty so callers can treat unpinned and pinned runs uniformly.
58	    pub fn core_for(&self, thread_idx: usize) -> Option<usize> {
59	        if self.pin_cores.is_empty() {
60	            None
61	        } else {
62	            Some(self.pin_cores[thread_idx % self.pin_cores.len()])
63	        }
64	    }
65	}
66	
67	/// Drive `bench` against `cfg` and return
68	/// `(histogram, outer, inner, duration_s)`.
69	///
70	/// After a fixed warmup, `inner` is auto-sized so apparatus framing
71	/// doesn't dominate (skipped when `cfg.inner_override` is set). The
72	/// outer loop runs either for `cfg.outer_override` iterations or
73	/// until `cfg.target_seconds` elapses.
74	pub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64, f64) {
75	    for _ in 0..WARMUP {
76	        black_box(bench.step());
77	    }
78	
79	    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);
80	    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);
81	    let inner = cfg
82	        .inner_override
83	        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns));
84	
85	    match cfg.outer_override {
86	        Some(outer) => {
87	            let (hist, duration_s) = run_counted(bench, outer, inner);
88	            (hist, outer, inner, duration_s)
89	        }
90	        None => {
91	            let (hist, duration_s) = run_timed(bench, cfg.target_seconds, inner);
92	            let outer = hist.len();
93	            (hist, outer, inner, duration_s)
94	        }
95	    }
96	}
97	
98	fn estimate_step_cost<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B) -> f64 {
99	    let mut samples: Vec<f64> = (0..ESTIMATE_SAMPLES)
100	        .map(|_| {
101	            let start = minstant::Instant::now();
102	            for _ in 0..ESTIMATE_STEPS {
103	                black_box(bench.step());
104	            }
105	            let total = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
106	            total as f64 / ESTIMATE_STEPS as f64
107	        })
108	        .collect();
109	    samples.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());
110	    samples[ESTIMATE_SAMPLES / 2]
111	}
112	
113	fn pick_inner(step_cost_ns: f64, framing_ns: f64) -> u64 {
114	    let target = (FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO * framing_ns / step_cost_ns).ceil() as u64;
115	    target.clamp(1, MAX_INNER)
116	}
117	
118	fn run_counted<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, outer: u64, inner: u64) -> (Histogram<u64>, f64) {
119	    let mut hist = new_hist();
120	    let run_start = minstant::Instant::now();
121	    for _ in 0..outer {
122	        record_sample(bench, inner, &mut hist);
123	    }
124	    let duration_s = run_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as f64 / 1e9;
125	    (hist, duration_s)
126	}
127	
128	fn run_timed<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, target_seconds: f64, inner: u64) -> (Histogram<u64>, f64) {
129	    let mut hist = new_hist();
130	    let target_ns = (target_seconds * 1e9) as u128;
131	    let run_start = minstant::Instant::now();
132	    loop {
133	        record_sample(bench, inner, &mut hist);
134	        if run_start.elapsed().as_nanos() >= target_ns {
135	            break;
136	        }
137	    }
138	    let duration_s = run_start.elapsed().as_nanos() as f64 / 1e9;
139	    (hist, duration_s)
140	}
141	
142	fn new_hist() -> Histogram<u64> {
143	    // 1 ns to 60 s, 3 sig figs
144	    Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 60_000_000_000, 3).unwrap()
145	}
146	
147	fn record_sample<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, inner: u64, hist: &mut Histogram<u64>) {
148	    let start = minstant::Instant::now();
149	    for _ in 0..inner {
150	        black_box(bench.step());
151	    }
152	    let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
153	    hist.record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns, inner)).unwrap();
154	}
155	
156	fn round_elapsed(elapsed_ns: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {
157	    (elapsed_ns + inner / 2) / inner
158	}
159	
160	/// Format an integer with thousands separators, e.g.
161	/// `12345` → `"12,345"`.
162	pub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {
163	    let s = n.to_string();
164	    let mut result = String::new();
165	    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {
166	        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {
167	            result.push(',');
168	        }
169	        result.push(c);
170	    }
171	    result.chars().rev().collect()
172	}
173	
174	/// Format a float with `decimals` fractional digits and thousands
175	/// separators on the integer part.
176	pub fn fmt_commas_f64(n: f64, decimals: usize) -> String {
177	    let s = format!("{n:.decimals$}");
178	    let (sign, body) = match s.strip_prefix('-') {
179	        Some(rest) => ("-", rest),
180	        None => ("", s.as_str()),
181	    };
182	    let (int_part, frac_part) = match body.find('.') {
183	        Some(i) => (&body[..i], &body[i..]),
184	        None => (body, ""),
185	    };
186	    let int_num: u64 = int_part.parse().unwrap_or(0);
187	    format!("{sign}{}{frac_part}", fmt_commas(int_num))
188	}
189	
190	/// Print the full bench report: header line (logfmt-style metadata),
191	/// per-band histogram (min→p1, p1→p10, … p99→max), whole-histogram
192	/// mean/stdev, and trimmed mean/stdev (min–p99, excluding the p99→max
193	/// tail). The `adjusted` columns subtract per-call apparatus overhead
194	/// (`overhead.per_call_ns(inner)`); the untrimmed `stdev` is the
195	/// hdrhistogram-native stdev, which includes the ms-scale outliers
196	/// in the tail band.
197	pub fn print_report(
198	    name: &str,
199	    outer: u64,
200	    inner: u64,
201	    duration_s: f64,
202	    hist: &Histogram<u64>,
203	    overhead: &Overhead,
204	) {
205	    // Header line: bench name + logfmt-style metadata. `adj` is the
206	    // apparatus overhead subtracted from each sample downstream.
207	    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns(inner);
208	    let total = outer * inner;
209	    println!(
210	        "{name} [duration={:.1}s outer={} inner={} calls={} adj/call={}ns]:",
211	        duration_s,
212	        fmt_commas(outer),
213	        inner,
214	        fmt_commas(total),
215	        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),
216	    );
217	
218	    // Band boundaries defined by percentiles. Each consecutive pair
219	    // forms one band; we iterate the histogram to compute per-band
220	    // stats (first, last, count, mean).
221	    let boundary_pcts: &[f64] = &[
222	        0.0, 0.01, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.99, 1.0,
223	    ];
224	    let boundary_names: &[&str] = &[
225	        "min", "p1", "p10", "p20", "p30", "p40", "p50", "p60", "p70", "p80", "p90", "p99", "max",
226	    ];
227	
228	    let n_bands = boundary_pcts.len() - 1;
229	    let sample_count = hist.len();
230	
231	    // Accumulate per-band stats by walking recorded histogram buckets.
232	    // Each bucket is assigned to the band containing its midpoint rank.
233	    let mut band_first = vec![u64::MAX; n_bands];
234	    let mut band_last = vec![0u64; n_bands];
235	    let mut band_count = vec![0u64; n_bands];
236	    let mut band_sum = vec![0u128; n_bands];
237	
238	    let mut cumulative = 0u64;
239	    for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {
240	        let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
241	        let count = iv.count_at_value();
242	        let mid_rank = (cumulative as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
243	        let idx = boundary_pcts[1..]
244	            .iter()
245	            .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
246	            .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
247	        band_first[idx] = band_first[idx].min(value);
248	        band_last[idx] = band_last[idx].max(value);
249	        band_count[idx] += count;
250	        band_sum[idx] += value as u128 * count as u128;
251	        cumulative += count;
252	    }
253	
254	    // Build rendered rows: (label, first, last, range, count, mean, adj_mean).
255	    struct BandRow {
256	        label: String,
257	        first: String,
258	        last: String,
259	        range: String,
260	        count: String,
261	        mean: String,
262	        adj_mean: String,
263	    }
264	
265	    let mut rows: Vec<BandRow> = Vec::new();
266	    for i in 0..n_bands {
267	        if band_count[i] == 0 {
268	            continue;
269	        }
270	        let mean_val = band_sum[i] as f64 / band_count[i] as f64;
271	        let adj_mean = (mean_val - adj).max(0.0);
272	        rows.push(BandRow {
273	            label: format!("{}-{}", boundary_names[i], boundary_names[i + 1]),
274	            first: fmt_commas(band_first[i]),
275	            last: fmt_commas(band_last[i]),
276	            range: fmt_commas(band_last[i] - band_first[i] + 1),
277	            count: fmt_commas(band_count[i]),
278	            mean: fmt_commas_f64(mean_val, 0),
279	            adj_mean: fmt_commas_f64(adj_mean, 0),
280	        });
281	    }
282	
283	    // Column widths from rendered strings.
284	    let label_w = rows
285	        .iter()
286	        .map(|r| r.label.len())
287	        .max()
288	        .unwrap_or(0)
289	        .max("stdev min-p99".len());
290	    let first_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.first.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
291	    let last_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.last.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
292	    let range_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.range.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
293	    let count_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.count.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
294	    let mean_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
295	    let adj_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.adj_mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
296	
297	    const INDENT: &str = "  ";
298	    const GAP: &str = "    ";
299	
300	    // Header row.
301	    let first_col = INDENT.len() + label_w + 1 + first_w;
302	    let last_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + last_w;
303	    let range_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + range_w;
304	    let count_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + count_w;
305	    let mean_gap = GAP.len() + mean_w;
306	    let adj_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + adj_w;
307	    println!(
308	        "{:>first_col$}{:>last_gap$}{:>range_gap$}{:>count_gap$}{:>mean_gap$}{:>adj_gap$}",
309	        "first", "last", "range", "count", "mean", "adjusted",
310	    );
311	
312	    for r in &rows {
313	        println!(
314	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>first_w$} ns{GAP}{:>last_w$} ns{GAP}{:>range_w$} ns{GAP}{:>count_w$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns{GAP}{:>adj_w$} ns",
315	            r.label, r.first, r.last, r.range, r.count, r.mean, r.adj_mean,
316	        );
317	    }
318	
319	    // Whole-histogram summary. Aligned to mean/adjusted columns.
320	    let hist_mean = hist.mean();
321	    let hist_adj = (hist_mean - adj).max(0.0);
322	    let skip = first_w
323	        + " ns".len()
324	        + GAP.len()
325	        + last_w
326	        + " ns".len()
327	        + GAP.len()
328	        + range_w
329	        + " ns".len()
330	        + GAP.len()
331	        + count_w;
332	    println!(
333	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns{GAP}{:>adj_w$} ns",
334	        "mean",
335	        "",
336	        fmt_commas_f64(hist_mean, 0),
337	        fmt_commas_f64(hist_adj, 0),
338	    );
339	    println!(
340	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns",
341	        "stdev",
342	        "",
343	        fmt_commas_f64(hist.stdev(), 0),
344	    );
345	
346	    // Trimmed mean/stdev (min–p99, excluding p99-max band).
347	    let trim_count: u64 = band_count[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
348	    if trim_count > 0 {
349	        let trim_sum: u128 = band_sum[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
350	        let trim_mean = trim_sum as f64 / trim_count as f64;
351	        let trim_adj = (trim_mean - adj).max(0.0);
352	
353	        // Variance: walk histogram buckets, include only non-tail bands.
354	        let mut trim_var_sum = 0.0f64;
355	        let mut trim_var_count = 0u64;
356	        let mut cum = 0u64;
357	        for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {
358	            let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
359	            let count = iv.count_at_value();
360	            let mid_rank = (cum as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
361	            let idx = boundary_pcts[1..]
362	                .iter()
363	                .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
364	                .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
365	            if idx < n_bands - 1 {
366	                let diff = value as f64 - trim_mean;
367	                trim_var_sum += diff * diff * count as f64;
368	                trim_var_count += count;
369	            }
370	            cum += count;
371	        }
372	        let trim_stdev = if trim_var_count > 1 {
373	            (trim_var_sum / trim_var_count as f64).sqrt()
374	        } else {
375	            0.0
376	        };
377	
378	        println!(
379	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns{GAP}{:>adj_w$} ns",
380	            "mean min-p99",
381	            "",
382	            fmt_commas_f64(trim_mean, 0),
383	            fmt_commas_f64(trim_adj, 0),
384	        );
385	        println!(
386	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns",
387	            "stdev min-p99",
388	            "",
389	            fmt_commas_f64(trim_stdev, 0),
390	        );
391	    }
392	    println!();
393	}
394	
```

1	//! Single-threaded `std::sync::mpsc` round-trip bench.
2	
3	use std::hint::black_box;
4	use std::sync::mpsc;
5	
6	use crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};
7	
8	/// Registry name used on the CLI.
9	pub const NAME: &str = "mpsc-1t";
10	
11	/// Same-thread send-then-receive through an `std::sync::mpsc`
12	/// channel. Measures pure channel overhead with no scheduler
13	/// interaction.
14	pub struct StdMpscRoundTrip {
15	    tx: mpsc::Sender<u64>,
16	    rx: mpsc::Receiver<u64>,
17	    counter: u64,
18	}
19	
20	impl StdMpscRoundTrip {
21	    /// Construct the bench with a fresh unbounded channel.
22	    pub fn new() -> Self {
23	        let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
24	        Self { tx, rx, counter: 0 }
25	    }
26	}
27	
28	impl Bench for StdMpscRoundTrip {
29	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
30	        "std::sync::mpsc round-trip (1 thread)"
31	    }
32	
33	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
34	        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);
35	        self.tx.send(self.counter).unwrap();
36	        let v = self.rx.recv().unwrap();
37	        black_box(v)
38	    }
39	}
40	
41	/// Registry entry point.
42	pub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {
43	    let mut bench = StdMpscRoundTrip::new();
44	    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);
45	    harness::print_report(bench.name(), outer, inner, duration_s, &hist, cfg.overhead);
46	}
47	
```

1	mod band_table;
2	mod benches;
3	mod harness;
4	mod overhead;
5	mod pin;
6	mod probe;
7	mod ticks;
8	mod tprobe;
9	mod tprobe2;
10	
11	use clap::Parser;
12	use log::{debug, info};
13	
14	#[derive(Parser)]
15	#[command(
16	    version,
17	    about = concat!(
18	        "iiac-perf ",
19	        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
20	        " — Rust latency microbenchmark harness",
21	    ),
22	    max_term_width = 80,
23	)]
24	struct Cli {
25	    /// Benches to run. Pass 'all' for every registered bench, or
26	    /// one or more names; a name matching no bench exactly runs
27	    /// every bench it is a prefix of (e.g. 'ice', 'mpsc').
28	    /// Run with no args to see the available list.
29	    benches: Vec<String>,
30	
31	    /// Target wall-clock seconds per bench (default 5.0). Auto-sizes
32	    /// outer and inner loop counts. Mutually exclusive with -D.
33	    #[arg(short = 'd', long, conflicts_with = "total_duration")]
34	    duration: Option<f64>,
35	
36	    /// Target total wall-clock seconds across all requested benches;
37	    /// budget is split equally per bench. Mutually exclusive with -d.
38	    #[arg(short = 'D', long)]
39	    total_duration: Option<f64>,
40	
41	    /// Override outer loop count (skips auto-sizing; inner still adapts).
42	    #[arg(short, long)]
43	    outer: Option<u64>,
44	
45	    /// Override inner loop count (skips auto-sizing).
46	    /// inner=1 measures single-call latency (each sample = one step); higher
47	    /// inner measures back-to-back/burst rate (each sample = N steps averaged).
48	    #[arg(short, long)]
49	    inner: Option<u64>,
50	
51	    /// Pin bench threads to logical CPUs (comma-separated, ranges OK).
52	    /// The list is a core *pool*: thread `i` of a bench is pinned to
53	    /// `pool[i % pool.len()]`, so shorter pools oversubscribe by wrap.
54	    /// Examples: `--pin 0,1` (2 threads → 2 CPUs), `--pin 0-5` (6-thread
55	    /// pool), `--pin 0,0` (two threads on the same CPU). On 3900X,
56	    /// logical CPUs N and N+12 are SMT siblings of the same physical
57	    /// core — `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max contention), `--pin 0,1`
58	    /// gives independent cores. Omit to leave threads unpinned.
59	    #[arg(long, value_name = "CORES")]
60	    pin: Option<String>,
61	
62	    /// Skip pinning the main thread for calibration. Always takes
63	    /// effect, including when `--pin` is set — bench threads still
64	    /// pin per `--pin`, but main stays on whatever affinity mask
65	    /// the process launched with. By default (without this flag),
66	    /// calibration runs with main pinned to `pin[0]` if set, else
67	    /// core 0, so framing/loop come from a stable environment.
68	    /// Pass this flag to decouple the calibration environment from
69	    /// the bench pinning pool (or to reproduce pre-0.6.0 behavior
70	    /// when `--pin` is absent).
71	    #[arg(long)]
72	    no_pin_cal: bool,
73	
74	    /// Enable verbose internals on stderr: affinity mask, cal
75	    /// parameters and raw fit inputs, calibration wall time.
76	    /// Equivalent to `RUST_LOG=debug`. Default is `warn` (silent
77	    /// unless something's wrong). `RUST_LOG` overrides this flag
78	    /// when set, so per-module filtering still works.
79	    #[arg(short, long)]
80	    verbose: bool,
81	
82	    /// Show tprobe results in raw TSC ticks instead of converting
83	    /// to nanoseconds. Only affects `TProbe` output; `Probe`
84	    /// results are always in nanoseconds.
85	    #[arg(short = 't', long)]
86	    ticks: bool,
87	}
88	
89	const DEFAULT_DURATION: f64 = 5.0;
90	
91	fn main() {
92	    let cli = Cli::parse();
93	
94	    // Default filter is `warn`; `-v` bumps to `debug`. `RUST_LOG`
95	    // (if set) always wins — so users can still do fine-grained
96	    // per-module filtering without fighting the flag.
97	    let mut builder = env_logger::Builder::from_default_env();
98	    if std::env::var_os("RUST_LOG").is_none() {
99	        builder.filter_level(if cli.verbose {
100	            log::LevelFilter::Debug
101	        } else {
102	            log::LevelFilter::Warn
103	        });
104	    }
105	    builder.format_timestamp(None).init();
106	
107	    if cli.benches.is_empty() {
108	        println!("no benches specified. use -h or --help for more info.");
109	        println!("available: all, {}", benches::names().join(", "));
110	        return;
111	    }
112	
113	    println!(
114	        "iiac-perf {} — Rust latency microbenchmark harness\n",
115	        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")
116	    );
117	
118	    if let Some(mask) = pin::current_affinity() {
119	        info!("startup affinity: {}", pin::affinity_summary(&mask));
120	    }
121	
122	    let pin_cores: Vec<usize> = match cli.pin.as_deref() {
123	        None => Vec::new(),
124	        Some(spec) => match pin::parse_cores(spec) {
125	            Ok(v) => v,
126	            Err(e) => {
127	                eprintln!("error: --pin: {e}");
128	                std::process::exit(2);
129	            }
130	        },
131	    };
132	
133	    // Pin main for calibration. Always pinned by default so
134	    // framing/loop numbers come from a stable environment
135	    // regardless of --pin. --no-pin-cal restores pre-0.6.0
136	    // behavior (pin iff --pin given).
137	    //
138	    // When we're doing the cal-only pin (--pin absent, --no-pin-cal
139	    // absent), snapshot the pre-cal affinity and restore it after
140	    // calibrate() so the scheduler regains freedom to co-locate
141	    // bench threads — otherwise main's pin leaks into the
142	    // scheduler's placement decisions and suppresses the
143	    // "both ends hot and spinning" fast path.
144	    let cal_pin: Option<usize> = if cli.no_pin_cal {
145	        None
146	    } else {
147	        pin_cores.first().copied().or(Some(0))
148	    };
149	    // Save/restore pre-cal affinity whenever we're about to set a
150	    // cal pin that wasn't the user's explicit bench-pin request —
151	    // i.e. the cal-only pin (--pin absent, --no-pin-cal absent).
152	    // --pin ⇒ user already wants main on pin[0]; leave alone.
153	    // --no-pin-cal ⇒ we aren't pinning at all; nothing to save.
154	    let saved_affinity = if pin_cores.is_empty() && !cli.no_pin_cal {
155	        pin::save_affinity()
156	    } else {
157	        None
158	    };
159	    pin::pin_current(cal_pin);
160	
161	    if let Some(cpu) = cal_pin {
162	        info!("pinned main to core {cpu} for calibration");
163	    } else {
164	        info!("cal pin skipped");
165	    }
166	    if let Some(mask) = pin::current_affinity() {
167	        debug!("affinity during cal: {}", pin::affinity_summary(&mask));
168	    }
169	
170	    let amp_coeff = overhead::N_HIGH as f64 / (overhead::N_HIGH - overhead::N_LOW) as f64;
171	    info!(
172	        "calibration params: warmup={}, samples={}, N_LOW={}, N_HIGH={}, noise_amp={:.4}",
173	        overhead::CAL_WARMUP,
174	        overhead::CAL_SAMPLES,
175	        overhead::N_LOW,
176	        overhead::N_HIGH,
177	        amp_coeff,
178	    );
179	
180	    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();
181	
182	    debug!(
183	        "calibration raw: min_low={} ns, min_high={} ns",
184	        overhead.cal_min_low_ns, overhead.cal_min_high_ns,
185	    );
186	    debug!(
187	        "calibration fit: framing={:.4} ns, loop_per_iter={:.4} ns",
188	        overhead.framing_per_sample_ns, overhead.loop_per_iter_ns,
189	    );
190	    info!(
191	        "calibration wall time: {:.2} ms",
192	        overhead.cal_duration.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0
193	    );
194	
195	    if let Some(set) = saved_affinity.as_ref() {
196	        pin::restore_affinity(set);
197	    }
198	
199	    let cal_pin_display = match (pin_cores.first(), cli.no_pin_cal) {
200	        (_, true) => "none (--no-pin-cal)".to_string(),
201	        (Some(c), false) => format!("core {c} (from --pin)"),
202	        (None, false) => "core 0 (unpinned after cal; --no-pin-cal to skip)".to_string(),
203	    };
204	    println!("Calibration:");
205	    println!(
206	        "  framing/sample    {:>7} ns  (timer pair, two-point fit)",
207	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.framing_per_sample_ns, 2)
208	    );
209	    println!(
210	        "  loop/iter         {:>7} ns  (per inner-loop iteration)",
211	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.loop_per_iter_ns, 2)
212	    );
213	    println!("  cal pin           {cal_pin_display}");
214	    println!("  bench pin         {}", pin::plan_summary(&pin_cores));
215	    println!();
216	
217	    let runners = match benches::resolve(&cli.benches) {
218	        Ok(r) => r,
219	        Err(e) => {
220	            eprintln!("error: {e}");
221	            std::process::exit(2);
222	        }
223	    };
224	
225	    let target_seconds = match (cli.duration, cli.total_duration) {
226	        (Some(d), _) => d,
227	        (None, Some(t)) => t / runners.len() as f64,
228	        (None, None) => DEFAULT_DURATION,
229	    };
230	
231	    let cfg = harness::RunCfg {
232	        overhead: &overhead,
233	        target_seconds,
234	        outer_override: cli.outer,
235	        inner_override: cli.inner,
236	        pin_cores: &pin_cores,
237	        report_ticks: cli.ticks,
238	    };
239	
240	    for run in runners {
241	        run(&cfg);
242	    }
243	}
244	
```

# Versioning

How this project versions its commits and the running
artifact. The scheme — the cycle step-numbering and the
unique-per-commit aim — is generic and shared across projects:
this file is copied **verbatim**, with
[Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
covering each medium by conditional rather than per-project
edits.

## Terms

Three names, used as defined here across
[AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md),
[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md), and the notes files:

- **version** — the per-commit version (e.g. `0.3.0-5.3.0`),
  written with `-` in prose, ladders, chores, todo, and commit
  talk; its suffix encodes the cycle phase (see
  [Step numbering](#step-numbering)).
- **version-of-record** — the authoritative stored copy of the
  version, in the project's manifest (see
  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record));
  a built or running artifact derives from it.
- **versioning** — the topic: this scheme as a whole.

## Recording the version-of-record

Where the version-of-record lives, how it's stored and
surfaced, and how often it changes — pick the case that fits
your medium:

- **Manifest** — where the version-of-record is stored:
  - if Rust, `Cargo.toml` `[package].version`
  - if Python, `pyproject.toml` `[project].version` (or the
    committed config it's sourced from)
  - otherwise wherever the medium records it (a generic
    `version.toml`, a book's frontmatter, …) — add the case as
    needed
- **Notation** — how the `-` form is stored:
  - if the format allows `-` (TOML `version.toml`, `Cargo.toml`),
    store it verbatim
  - if it bars `-` (PEP 440's local segment, e.g. a Python
    project), remap to `+` — `0.3.0-5.3.0` → `0.3.0+5.3.0`: same
    version, just the stored spelling
- **Reporter** — how a built artifact surfaces the
  version-of-record:
  - if a CLI app, `<cli-app> -V`
  - if a TUI/GUI, add to Help/About or display on the title
- **Cadence** — how often to bump: see
  [Unique per commit](#unique-per-commit-preference-not-requirement);
  this project follows the per-commit preference.

## Unique per commit (preference, not requirement)

Our general notion is that the version-of-record should change
on **every commit**, so a built or running artifact identifies
the exact commit it came from.

- This is a preference, **not** a hard requirement. A project
  following the cycle protocol may bump less often — once per
  cycle, only at release, and so on. Record the choice in
  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
  if it differs.
- It is achievable because the cycle's versions (below) are
  **pre-assignable**, unlike the git SHA, which a commit cannot
  contain (see the cycle protocol's
  [Commits backfill](cycle-protocol.md#commits-backfill)).

## Step numbering

The cycle (Preparation -> Work -> Close-out; see
[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md)) encodes each commit's
phase in the version suffix — the **final identifier `0` marks
a Preparation**:

- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)

**Preparation is optional.** A lightweight cycle — no ladder,
no setup commit — skips `-0` and starts at `-1` (its first
Work commit). The same holds at every level: a sub-cycle
needing no Preparation omits its `.0` (see Nesting). One that
grows a Preparation later adds the `0` step without
renumbering siblings.

Disambiguation:

- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
  Preparation.
- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
  identifier `0`).

**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:

- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle
```

> AGENT

Good idea — it reaches the same end state as my one-shot rebase but through a cleaner recovery pattern, and it exercises machinery worth testing. My thoughts on the sequence:

1. **`jj new main -R .`** — moves `@` to a fresh empty commit on top of `mpwknwnk`. The prep edits aren't lost: `vuupwuvr` stays behind as a real commit (a leaf off the `0.11.0` base) since in jj the working copy is always a commit. This cleanly separates "stand on a current base" from "bring the old work over".

2. **Sync** — I'd want to confirm what you mean: `vc-x1 sync` (which preflight runs as `--check`) or a plain `jj git fetch`? Either way it should be a **no-op here** — local `main`, `main@git`, and `main@origin` all already point at `436b6f61`, so there's nothing to fetch. That's actually the nice part of the test: we verify sync correctly reports "already current" rather than doing something surprising. If it *does* pull something newer, the next step just targets the true tip — which is exactly why your ordering is more robust than my direct rebase.

3. **`jj rebase -r vuupwuvr -d main -R .`** — moves the prep commit onto main. Its change ID survives (that's jj's core property), and it'll come out **conflicted**: `Cargo.toml` (both sides edited the `version` line) and `Cargo.lock` for sure; `notes/todo.md` should auto-merge since the aarch64 commit touched Done/refs and I touched In Progress — a good little three-way-merge corner to observe.

4. **`jj edit -r vuupwuvr -R .`** — make it the working copy again to resolve. The empty `@` from step 1 gets auto-abandoned by jj when we leave it (empty + undescribed), so no litter. Resolution plan: restore `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` from main's side, re-bump to `0.13.0-0`, `cargo check` to regenerate the lock, verify todo.md merged clean.

Then the follow-ups regardless of route: renumber the ladder `0.12.0-*` → `0.13.0-*` in todo.md, and check `vc-x1 push --status` before re-pushing — the rollback may have left a stale `push-state.toml`, so `--restart` is likely needed.

One small alternative for step 3+4: `jj edit` the conflicted commit directly is simplest; squashing `vuupwuvr` into the new empty `@` would also work but adds a step for no gain.

Two corners this tests that the direct rebase wouldn't: sync-when-current, and rebase-of-a-detached-working-copy-commit with conflict materialization. Happy to run it in that order when you say go.

I'm think .claude too but since .claude is "always" as linear commit list and each line of a jsonl file is unique there shouldn't be any conflicts so maybe it's not necessary to jj new main -R .claude. I think it's be worth while to do the experiment if you also think they'll be no conficits in the .claude repo. Thoughts only!

> AGENT

Good instinct to think about `.claude` too — it likely has the *same divergence* (the rpi5 session would have pushed its session commit, `vkzkxnwzkozp`, to the `.claude` remote, and this machine never fetched). But my analysis splits into two parts:

**On conflicts — you're mostly right, with one correction to the reasoning.** Merge conflicts are per-*file*, not per-line: the jsonl journals are named by session UUID, so the rpi5 session's files and this machine's files are disjoint filenames — they can't conflict no matter what their lines contain. Line-uniqueness never enters into it. The actual conflict surface is the **shared mutable files**: `settings.json` / `settings.local.json`. You edited settings today on this machine (the `../zc-ring-x1` read permission); if the rpi5 session's pushed commit also touched those files, that's a real same-file three-way merge — possibly clean (different regions), possibly not. So "no conflicts" is *probable*, not guaranteed. A read-only `jj bookmark list --all-remotes -R .claude` + a peek at what the rpi5 commit touched would settle it before deciding.

**On `jj new main -R .claude` — I'd advise against that specific move, for a reason unrelated to conflicts.** `.claude`'s working copy is the **live journal**: the harness is appending to this session's jsonl *right now*. `jj new main` checks out main's tree, which removes from disk any files that exist only in the current `@` — including the live session file. The harness's open file handle would keep writing to an unlinked inode; when we `jj edit` back, the file is restored from the last snapshot and the in-flight appends are gone. That's exactly the fragility that `finalize --delay 10` exists to protect against, and we'd be triggering it deliberately mid-session.

**Gentler experiment that still tests the corner:** fetch first (`jj git fetch -R .claude` or whatever `vc-x1 sync` covers), then an **in-place** `jj rebase -d main -R .claude` of `@` — no checkout-away window: unchanged files stay put on disk, the rpi5 session's disjoint files get added, and only a genuine settings conflict would materialize markers. That exercises sync-then-rebase-live-journal without the data-loss window.

If you want the full `jj new` dance in `.claude` for completeness, the safe moment is right after a session-ending finalize (journal quiescent), not mid-session.

So my proposal: app repo gets your full `jj new main` → sync → rebase → edit sequence; `.claude` gets fetch → recon → in-place rebase. Say go and I'll start with the read-only recon of both repos.
