> AGENT

My thoughts, in order of what would actually decide this:

**We're missing a cheap fact that might make the whole question moot.** Here's a clue: the failed push's preflight ran `vc-x1 sync --check` and *passed*. And the app repo's `main@origin` tracking ref was already at `436b6f61` locally — meaning this machine *did* fetch the rpi5 work at some point (fetch updates tracking refs but never moves `@`, which is why our working copy stayed parented on `0.11.0`). If that fetch covered `.claude` too, its local `main` may already equal origin, and `.claude`'s `@` might just need an in-place rebase — or nothing at all if it's already parented on the current tip. Five minutes of read-only recon (`jj log` / `jj bookmark list --all-remotes -R .claude`, plus a `--stat` of the rpi5 `.claude` commit to see if it touches `settings.*`) answers everything before we choose a strategy.

**On the settings-swap idea — I'd skip it, for three reasons:**

1. It may be solving a non-problem: if the rpi5 commit didn't touch `settings.json`/`settings.local.json`, there's no conflict to preempt.
2. Even if there *is* a conflict, the swap is manual conflict resolution done early, with a worse result: restoring the old files so the rebase merges clean means the rebased history carries the *old* settings, and you then re-apply today's grants by hand. Letting jj materialize the conflict and resolving it as a **union** (both machines' permission grants) is the same work with the better outcome — and it's the more interesting corner to test, frankly.
3. Your LoL-worry is legitimate but mild: the harness reads permissions per-tool-call and *writes* `settings.local.json` when grants happen mid-session. Restoring old versions wouldn't crash me — worst case is I start getting permission prompts again — but a mid-session harness write could silently clobber your swap and muddy the experiment. Another reason to leave those files alone and let the merge handle it.

**On Option A (`jj new -R .claude` + `/exit` + manual sync):** that's the right way to do it *if* you want that specific experiment — a quiescent journal is the only safe time. But I'd hold it in reserve rather than plan on it; if recon shows `.claude` is current or trivially rebasable in place, you keep the session and we get on with the benches.

**One thing recon must confirm regardless:** `finalize-claude` pushes `.claude main` at the tail of every `vc-x1 push`, so if `.claude` *is* diverged, it must be sorted before the next push attempt — it would fail the same way the app repo did, just at the last stage instead.

So my recommendation: I do the read-only recon of `.claude` next (no mutations, no sync yet), report what's actually there, and then you pick between "in-place rebase, union-merge settings if needed" and "/exit + manual". Want me to look?

Recon complete. Report:

**`.claude` divergence — same shape as the app repo, already fully fetched.** Local `main` == `main@git` == `main@origin` == `vkzkxnwz` (the rpi5 session commit — it's the exact chid the app-repo `ochid: /.claude/vkzkxnwzkozp` trailer points at, so the cross-links are intact). Our session's `@` is parented on the `0.11.0` session commit, a sibling of main. So **sync will be a no-op in both repos** — everything is fetched; the only fix needed anywhere is rebasing the two `@`s onto their mains.

**Conflict forecast for `settings.local.json`: yes, one tiny conflict, trivially resolvable.** Both sides appended entries at the *same spot* (end of the `allow` array, right after `"WebFetch(domain:github.com)"`):

- rpi5 side added 1 entry: `"Bash(/home/nps-gnc/.cargo/bin/iiac-perf tp-pc *)"`
- our side added 5: the two `zc-ring-x1` Read grants, `cargo check`, `git ls-remote`, `jj show`

Same-anchor insertions conflict in a three-way merge, but the union resolution (keep all six lines) is obvious and mechanical. The jsonl session files are disjoint as you predicted (`53984af8…` theirs, `75b85bd0…`/`93ce1ca2…` ours) — no conflict possible there. `settings.json` is a new file only on our side — no conflict.

**Bonus finding — your new `settings.json` has a JSON syntax error.** Line 26: `"Bash(python3 *)"` is missing the trailing comma before the `Read(...zc-ring-x1...)` entry on the next line, so the whole file likely fails to parse and its grants are dead. The reason my `../zc-ring-x1` reads worked anyway is that the same Read grants also got added to `settings.local.json`. One-character fix.

**Recommendation:** no `/exit`, no settings swap needed. The plan:

1. App repo: your sequence — `jj new main`, `vc-x1 sync` (expect "already current"), `jj rebase -r vuupwuvr -d main`, `jj edit vuupwuvr`, resolve Cargo.toml/lock, renumber to `0.13.0`.
2. `.claude`: in-place `jj rebase -d main -R .claude` (no `jj new`, journal stays live), union-resolve the one settings.local.json conflict.
3. Fix the settings.json comma (or you do it — your call, it's your config).
4. `vc-x1 push main --restart` with the corrected `0.13.0-0` title/body.

vc-x1 0.67.0

Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.

Repo set is resolved (in order):
  - `-R` / `--repo`     exact list (back-compat / arbitrary multi-repo)
  - `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` dual-repo roles via `.vc-config.toml`
  - neither             default: `code,bot` when dual, else `code`

One atomic operation: fetch, then per repo:
  - up-to-date        nothing to do
  - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote
  - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)
  - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts
  - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip

After a successful sync, `@` is repositioned onto the synced
bookmark: the code repo `jj new`s a clean `@` (or rebases a
dirty one with --rebase / a prompt), the `.claude` session
repo always `jj new main`s.

On failure sync stops where the failing step stopped — nothing
is auto-reverted, so the state can be inspected. Each repo's
pre-sync op id is persisted to `.vc-x1/sync-state.toml`; undo
explicitly with `vc-x1 revert` (state is cleared on success).

Output shape:
  - all-up-to-date: one-line summary (`sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`)
  - action needed:  per-repo fetch + state + actions
  - --quiet:        no output; exit code signals success

Usage: vc-x1 sync [OPTIONS]

Options:
  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
          
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

  -q, --quiet
          Suppress all informational output (exit code signals result)

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to sync in each repo
          
          [default: main]

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --remote <REMOTE>
          Remote to sync against
          
          [default: origin]

      --rebase
          Rebase a non-empty `@` onto the synced bookmark without asking.
          
          After a successful sync, `@` is repositioned onto the synced bookmark.
          When the code repo's `@` carries changes it normally asks before
          rebasing; `--rebase` answers yes up front for non-interactive use.
          Ignored for the session repo (which always `jj new main`).

  -R, --repo <PATH>
          Workspace root, or a single jj repo to sync on its own.
          
          - `-R PATH` alone — sync just the repo at PATH.
          - `-R PATH -s ROLES` — use PATH as the workspace root and
            sync the named side(s).

  -s, --scope <SCOPE>
          Which repo(s) of the workspace to sync.
          
          `SCOPE=code|bot|code,bot`:
          
          - `code` — sync only the app repo.
          - `bot` — sync only the bot repo (errors if no bot repo
            is configured).
          - `code,bot` — sync both repos.
          
          Composes with `-R` as the workspace root. Default depends
          on workspace state: dual workspace → `code,bot`;
          single-repo workspace or POR → `code`.

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/zcr_common.rs",
  "content": "//! Shared setup for the `zcr-*` benches: leaked ring regions\n//! and `'static` endpoint construction over the sibling\n//! `zc-ring-x1` crate.\n\nuse zc_ring_x1::{CACHE_LINE_SIZE, Consumer, Header, Producer, Ring};\n\n/// Slot payload for every zcr bench: the round-trip counter.\n/// `u64` satisfies the zerocopy bounds and matches the message\n/// shape of the mpsc/ice benches.\npub type Msg = u64;\n\n/// Shutdown sentinel the 2t benches send instead of a counter\n/// value; the echo worker exits on receipt without replying.\n/// The counter increments skip it (see each bench's `step`).\npub const STOP: Msg = u64::MAX;\n\n/// Slots per ring — a power of two, comfortably above the one\n/// message ever in flight in the round-trip benches.\npub const CAPACITY: u32 = 8;\n\n/// Region bytes: the four-cache-line [`Header`] plus\n/// [`CAPACITY`] slots of one cache line each.\nconst REGION_BYTES: usize = size_of::<Header>() + CACHE_LINE_SIZE * CAPACITY as usize;\n\n/// Cache-line-aligned backing region for one ring, matching\n/// `Ring::init`'s alignment requirement.\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\nstruct Region([u8; REGION_BYTES]);\n\n/// Build a ring over a leaked region and split it into\n/// `'static` endpoint handles.\n///\n/// - Leaked on purpose: the 2t benches move one endpoint into a\n///   spawned worker thread, so the region must outlive the\n///   bench struct. ~768 B per ring for the process lifetime is\n///   fine in a bench binary.\npub fn leak_ring() -> (Producer<'static>, Consumer<'static>) {\n    let region: &'static mut Region = Box::leak(Box::new(Region([0; REGION_BYTES])));\n    Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, CAPACITY)\n        .expect(\"geometry is valid by construction\")\n        .split()\n}\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/zcr_raw_1t.rs",
  "content": "//! Single-threaded zc-ring-x1 round-trip bench, raw API tier.\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Producer};\n\nuse crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, leak_ring};\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};\n\n/// Registry name used on the CLI.\npub const NAME: &str = \"zcr-raw-1t\";\n\n/// Same-thread reserve/commit then reserve/release through one\n/// zc-ring-x1 ring, using the raw `reserve_slot` tier.\n///\n/// - The raw tier surfaces `Full`/`Empty` to the caller; neither\n///   occurs here — exactly one message is in flight against a\n///   capacity-8 ring — so this measures pure ring overhead with\n///   no wait loop and no cross-core traffic.\n/// - Baseline for `zcr-with-1t` / `zcr-spin-1t`: any delta\n///   between the three is pure API-tier cost.\npub struct ZcrRaw1Thread {\n    producer: Producer<'static>,\n    consumer: Consumer<'static>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl ZcrRaw1Thread {\n    /// Construct the bench over one fresh leaked ring.\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (producer, consumer) = leak_ring();\n        Self {\n            producer,\n            consumer,\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for ZcrRaw1Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"zcr-raw-1t: zc-ring-x1 raw round-trip (1 thread)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        let mut slot = self\n            .producer\n            .reserve_slot::<Msg>()\n            .expect(\"never full: one message in flight\");\n        *slot = self.counter;\n        slot.commit();\n        let slot = self\n            .consumer\n            .reserve_slot::<Msg>()\n            .expect(\"never empty: message just committed\");\n        let v = *slot;\n        slot.release();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Registry entry point.\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let mut bench = ZcrRaw1Thread::new();\n    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);\n    harness::print_report(bench.name(), outer, inner, duration_s, &hist, cfg.overhead);\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/zcr_raw_2t.rs",
  "content": "//! Two-threaded zc-ring-x1 round-trip bench, raw API tier,\n//! spin waits.\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::thread;\n\nuse zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Empty, Full, Producer};\n\nuse crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, STOP, leak_ring};\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};\nuse crate::pin;\n\n/// Registry name used on the CLI.\npub const NAME: &str = \"zcr-raw-2t\";\n\n/// Main → worker → main round-trip over two zc-ring-x1 rings\n/// (req + resp), both ends using the raw `reserve_slot` tier\n/// with hand-written spin loops on `Full`/`Empty`.\n///\n/// - Same wait policy as `mpsc-2t-spin` / `ice-*-2t`\n///   (non-blocking attempt + `spin_loop`), so zcr-raw-2t vs\n///   those is a pure transport comparison, while zcr-raw-2t vs\n///   `zcr-with-2t` / `zcr-spin-2t` isolates API-tier cost under\n///   real cross-core traffic.\n/// - Shutdown: `Drop` sends the [`STOP`] sentinel; the worker\n///   exits on receipt without replying.\npub struct ZcrRaw2Thread {\n    req_tx: Producer<'static>,\n    resp_rx: Consumer<'static>,\n    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl ZcrRaw2Thread {\n    /// Spawn the spinning echo worker over two fresh leaked\n    /// rings, optionally pinning it to `worker_cpu`.\n    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {\n        let (req_tx, mut req_rx) = leak_ring();\n        let (mut resp_tx, resp_rx) = leak_ring();\n        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {\n            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);\n            loop {\n                let v = loop {\n                    match req_rx.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {\n                        Ok(slot) => {\n                            let v = *slot;\n                            slot.release();\n                            break v;\n                        }\n                        Err(Empty) => core::hint::spin_loop(),\n                    }\n                };\n                if v == STOP {\n                    break;\n                }\n                let mut slot = loop {\n                    match resp_tx.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {\n                        Ok(slot) => break slot,\n                        Err(Full) => core::hint::spin_loop(),\n                    }\n                };\n                *slot = v;\n                slot.commit();\n            }\n        });\n        Self {\n            req_tx,\n            resp_rx,\n            worker: Some(worker),\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for ZcrRaw2Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"zcr-raw-2t: zc-ring-x1 raw round-trip (2 threads, spin)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        if self.counter == STOP {\n            self.counter = 1;\n        }\n        let mut slot = self\n            .req_tx\n            .reserve_slot::<Msg>()\n            .expect(\"never full: one message in flight\");\n        *slot = self.counter;\n        slot.commit();\n        loop {\n            match self.resp_rx.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {\n                Ok(slot) => {\n                    let v = *slot;\n                    slot.release();\n                    return black_box(v);\n                }\n                Err(Empty) => core::hint::spin_loop(),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for ZcrRaw2Thread {\n    /// Send [`STOP`] and join the worker.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        let mut slot = self\n            .req_tx\n            .reserve_slot::<Msg>()\n            .expect(\"never full: nothing in flight at drop\");\n        *slot = STOP;\n        slot.commit();\n        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {\n            let _ = worker.join();\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Registry entry point.\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let mut bench = ZcrRaw2Thread::new(cfg.core_for(1));\n    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);\n    harness::print_report(bench.name(), outer, inner, duration_s, &hist, cfg.overhead);\n}\n"
}

1	//! Shipped wait policies — the models users copy.
2	//!
3	//! A wait policy is any `FnMut(u32) -> bool`: the `_with`
4	//! methods ([`Pool::alloc_with`](crate::Pool::alloc_with),
5	//! [`Producer::reserve_slot_with`](crate::Producer::reserve_slot_with),
6	//! [`Consumer::reserve_slot_with`](crate::Consumer::reserve_slot_with))
7	//! call it after each failed attempt with the attempt count
8	//! (0-based, saturating); returning `false` gives up and the
9	//! operation returns its usual error.
10	//!
11	//! - The crate ships only the policies its own demo and
12	//!   tests use; compose your own (bounded retries, deadline,
13	//!   spin-then-yield) as capturing closures at the call
14	//!   site, or bake one into your own wrapper method.
15	//! - Policies are polling strategies. True blocking (futex,
16	//!   eventfd, async wakers) needs a peer wake over the
17	//!   header's user line and is a layer above this crate.
18	
19	/// Spin forever: hint the CPU and keep waiting.
20	///
21	/// `#[inline]`: non-generic, so without the hint a
22	/// monomorphized `_with` in another crate calls it out of
23	/// line — measurable on the demo's ~3 ns loops.
24	#[inline]
25	pub fn spin(_attempt: u32) -> bool {
26	    core::hint::spin_loop();
27	    true
28	}
29

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/zcr_with_1t.rs",
  "content": "//! Single-threaded zc-ring-x1 round-trip bench, closure\n//! (`reserve_slot_with`) API tier.\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Producer};\n\nuse crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, leak_ring};\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};\n\n/// Registry name used on the CLI.\npub const NAME: &str = \"zcr-with-1t\";\n\n/// Same-thread round-trip as `zcr-raw-1t`, but reserving through\n/// `reserve_slot_with` with an app-supplied spin closure.\n///\n/// - The closure never runs here (one message in flight, never\n///   full/empty), so any delta vs `zcr-raw-1t` is the cost of\n///   the `_with` wrapper's fast path — which zc-ring-x1's docs\n///   claim does exactly the loads `reserve_slot` does.\npub struct ZcrWith1Thread {\n    producer: Producer<'static>,\n    consumer: Consumer<'static>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl ZcrWith1Thread {\n    /// Construct the bench over one fresh leaked ring.\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (producer, consumer) = leak_ring();\n        Self {\n            producer,\n            consumer,\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for ZcrWith1Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"zcr-with-1t: zc-ring-x1 reserve_slot_with round-trip (1 thread)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        let mut slot = self\n            .producer\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                core::hint::spin_loop();\n                true\n            })\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        *slot = self.counter;\n        slot.commit();\n        let slot = self\n            .consumer\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                core::hint::spin_loop();\n                true\n            })\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        let v = *slot;\n        slot.release();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Registry entry point.\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let mut bench = ZcrWith1Thread::new();\n    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);\n    harness::print_report(bench.name(), outer, inner, duration_s, &hist, cfg.overhead);\n}\n"
}
```

  "content": "//! Two-threaded zc-ring-x1 round-trip bench, closure\n//! (`reserve_slot_with`) API tier, spin waits.\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::thread;\n\nuse zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Producer};\n\nuse crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, STOP, leak_ring};\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};\nuse crate::pin;\n\n/// Registry name used on the CLI.\npub const NAME: &str = \"zcr-with-2t\";\n\n/// Main → worker → main round-trip over two zc-ring-x1 rings as\n/// `zcr-raw-2t`, but both ends wait inside `reserve_slot_with`\n/// with an app-supplied spin closure instead of a hand-written\n/// retry loop around `reserve_slot`.\n///\n/// - Same wait policy as `zcr-raw-2t` (spin_loop hint per failed\n///   attempt), so any delta between the two is the cost of the\n///   `_with` wrapper under real cross-core traffic.\n/// - Shutdown: `Drop` sends the [`STOP`] sentinel; the worker\n///   exits on receipt without replying.\npub struct ZcrWith2Thread {\n    req_tx: Producer<'static>,\n    resp_rx: Consumer<'static>,\n    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl ZcrWith2Thread {\n    /// Spawn the spinning echo worker over two fresh leaked\n    /// rings, optionally pinning it to `worker_cpu`.\n    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {\n        let (req_tx, mut req_rx) = leak_ring();\n        let (mut resp_tx, resp_rx) = leak_ring();\n        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {\n            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);\n            loop {\n                let v = {\n                    let slot = req_rx\n                        .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                            core::hint::spin_loop();\n                            true\n                        })\n                        .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n                    let v = *slot;\n                    slot.release();\n                    v\n                };\n                if v == STOP {\n                    break;\n                }\n                let mut slot = resp_tx\n                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                        core::hint::spin_loop();\n                        true\n                    })\n                    .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n                *slot = v;\n                slot.commit();\n            }\n        });\n        Self {\n            req_tx,\n            resp_rx,\n            worker: Some(worker),\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for ZcrWith2Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"zcr-with-2t: zc-ring-x1 reserve_slot_with round-trip (2 threads, spin)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        if self.counter == STOP {\n            self.counter = 1;\n        }\n        let mut slot = self\n            .req_tx\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                core::hint::spin_loop();\n                true\n            })\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        *slot = self.counter;\n        slot.commit();\n        let slot = self\n            .resp_rx\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                core::hint::spin_loop();\n                true\n            })\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        let v = *slot;\n        slot.release();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for ZcrWith2Thread {\n    /// Send [`STOP`] and join the worker.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        let mut slot = self\n            .req_tx\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                core::hint::spin_loop();\n                true\n            })\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        *slot = STOP;\n        slot.commit();\n        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {\n            let _ = worker.join();\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Registry entry point.\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let mut bench = ZcrWith2Thread::new(cfg.core_for(1));\n    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);\n    harness::print_report(bench.name(), outer, inner, duration_s, &hist, cfg.overhead);\n}\n"
}

  "content": "//! Single-threaded zc-ring-x1 round-trip bench, easy\n//! (`reserve_slot_spin`) API tier.\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Producer};\n\nuse crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, leak_ring};\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};\n\n/// Registry name used on the CLI.\npub const NAME: &str = \"zcr-spin-1t\";\n\n/// Same-thread round-trip as `zcr-raw-1t`, but reserving through\n/// `reserve_slot_spin` — the easy tier with the spin policy baked\n/// in and no `Result` to handle.\n///\n/// - The spin never engages here (one message in flight, never\n///   full/empty), so any delta vs `zcr-raw-1t` / `zcr-with-1t`\n///   is the cost of the built-in-policy wrapper's fast path.\npub struct ZcrSpin1Thread {\n    producer: Producer<'static>,\n    consumer: Consumer<'static>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl ZcrSpin1Thread {\n    /// Construct the bench over one fresh leaked ring.\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (producer, consumer) = leak_ring();\n        Self {\n            producer,\n            consumer,\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for ZcrSpin1Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"zcr-spin-1t: zc-ring-x1 reserve_slot_spin round-trip (1 thread)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        let mut slot = self.producer.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n        *slot = self.counter;\n        slot.commit();\n        let slot = self.consumer.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n        let v = *slot;\n        slot.release();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Registry entry point.\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let mut bench = ZcrSpin1Thread::new();\n    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);\n    harness::print_report(bench.name(), outer, inner, duration_s, &hist, cfg.overhead);\n}\n"
}

(`reserve_slot_spin`) API tier.\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::thread;\n\nuse zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Producer};\n\nuse crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, STOP, leak_ring};\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};\nuse crate::pin;\n\n/// Registry name used on the CLI.\npub const NAME: &str = \"zcr-spin-2t\";\n\n/// Main → worker → main round-trip over two zc-ring-x1 rings as\n/// `zcr-raw-2t`, but both ends wait inside `reserve_slot_spin` —\n/// the easy tier with the spin policy baked in.\n///\n/// - Same effective wait policy as `zcr-raw-2t` / `zcr-with-2t`\n///   (spin_loop hint per failed attempt), so any delta between\n///   the three is pure API-tier cost under cross-core traffic.\n/// - Shutdown: `Drop` sends the [`STOP`] sentinel; the worker\n///   exits on receipt without replying. `reserve_slot_spin`\n///   never gives up, so the sentinel (not a closure returning\n///   `false`) is the only way to unblock a spinning consumer.\npub struct ZcrSpin2Thread {\n    req_tx: Producer<'static>,\n    resp_rx: Consumer<'static>,\n    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl ZcrSpin2Thread {\n    /// Spawn the spinning echo worker over two fresh leaked\n    /// rings, optionally pinning it to `worker_cpu`.\n    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {\n        let (req_tx, mut req_rx) = leak_ring();\n        let (mut resp_tx, resp_rx) = leak_ring();\n        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {\n            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);\n            loop {\n                let slot = req_rx.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n                let v = *slot;\n                slot.release();\n                if v == STOP {\n                    break;\n                }\n                let mut slot = resp_tx.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n                *slot = v;\n                slot.commit();\n            }\n        });\n        Self {\n            req_tx,\n            resp_rx,\n            worker: Some(worker),\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for ZcrSpin2Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"zcr-spin-2t: zc-ring-x1 reserve_slot_spin round-trip (2 threads, spin)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        if self.counter == STOP {\n            self.counter = 1;\n        }\n        let mut slot = self.req_tx.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n        *slot = self.counter;\n        slot.commit();\n        let slot = self.resp_rx.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n        let v = *slot;\n        slot.release();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for ZcrSpin2Thread {\n    /// Send [`STOP`] and join the worker.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        let mut slot = self.req_tx.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n        *slot = STOP;\n        slot.commit();\n        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {\n            let _ = worker.join();\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Registry entry point.\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let mut bench = ZcrSpin2Thread::new(cfg.core_for(1));\n    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);\n    harness::print_report(bench.name(), outer, inner, duration_s, &hist, cfg.overhead);\n}\n"
}

## Example runs

All measurements below are on a Ryzen 9 3900X, idle desktop,
`mpsc-2t -d 3`. Numbers vary run-to-run and machine-to-machine;
the *shape* of the differences is the useful signal.

### `all` results (3900X, 0.11.0)

One `iiac-perf all` run (default 5 s per bench, unpinned, idle
desktop), adjusted mean per bench; probe-based benches report
their probes' unadjusted means. Same caveat as above: shapes,
not absolutes.

| bench             | adjusted mean | note                        |
|-------------------|--------------:|-----------------------------|
| min-now           |          8 ns | `minstant::Instant::now`    |
| std-now           |         21 ns | `std::time::Instant::now`   |
| mpsc-1t           |         25 ns | same-thread channel         |
| mpsc-2t           |      7,253 ns | blocking `recv` (park/wake) |
| mpsc-2t-spin      |        129 ns | spin `try_recv`             |
| probe-mpsc-2t     |      7,445 ns | probes: send 744 / 772 ns   |
| producer-consumer |             — | probes: 7,511 / 7,532 ns    |
| tp-pc             |             — | probes: 7,396 / 7,403 ns    |
| tp2-pc            |             — | probes: 7,320 / 7,326 ns    |
| ice-ps-1t         |        271 ns | iceoryx2 pub/sub, 1 thread  |
| ice-ps-2t         |        699 ns | iceoryx2 pub/sub, 2 threads |
| ice-rr-1t         |        800 ns | iceoryx2 req/res, 1 thread  |
| ice-rr-2t         |      1,118 ns | iceoryx2 req/res, 2 threads |

The wait-policy split dominates the 2-thread rows: the parking
benches (`mpsc-2t` and the probe family, all blocking `recv`)
cluster at ~7.3-7.5 µs while the spinning benches sit under
1.2 µs. For context, iceoryx2's own pub/sub benchmark (v0.9.2,
`--bench-all`) on this machine reports 250 ns one-way — ~500 ns
round-trip — with pinned realtime threads and untouched payloads,
consistent with `ice-ps-2t`'s 699 ns measured here.

### Verbose output (`-v`)

`-v` prints the affinity/calibration lifecycle on stderr. The
default cal policy is visible: startup mask → `save_affinity` →
pin main to core 0 → calibrate → `restore_affinity` → benches
run on the original (unpinned) mask.

```
$ iiac-perf mpsc-2t -d 3 -v
iiac-perf 0.7.0 — Rust latency microbenchmark harness

[INFO  iiac_perf] startup affinity: 0-23 (24 cpus)
[INFO  iiac_perf::pin] save_affinity: mask=0-23 (24 cpus)
[INFO  iiac_perf] pinned main to core 0 for calibration
[DEBUG iiac_perf] affinity during cal: 0 (1 cpu)
[INFO  iiac_perf] calibration params: warmup=100000, samples=100000, N_LOW=100, N_HIGH=10000, noise_amp=1.0101
[DEBUG iiac_perf] calibration raw: min_low=60 ns, min_high=4899 ns
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/notes/chores/chores-04.md",
  "old_string": "Commits: [[1]],[[2]],[[3]]\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "Commits: [[1]],[[2]],[[3]],[[4]]\n\n### Tier comparison (0.13.0-4)\n\nMethod: installed `iiac-perf 0.13.0-4`, Ryzen 9 3900X, idle\ndesktop, `iiac-perf zcr mpsc-2t-spin ice-ps-2t -d 5 --pin 0,1`;\nvalues are the adjusted trimmed mean (min-p99). Run 2 repeats\nthe 2t trio to check repeatability.\n\n| bench        | run 1  | run 2  |\n|--------------|-------:|-------:|\n| zcr-raw-1t   |   4 ns |      — |\n| zcr-with-1t  |   4 ns |      — |\n| zcr-spin-1t  |   5 ns |      — |\n| zcr-raw-2t   | 119 ns | 120 ns |\n| zcr-with-2t  | 101 ns | 101 ns |\n| zcr-spin-2t  | 101 ns |  99 ns |\n| mpsc-2t-spin | 117 ns |      — |\n| ice-ps-2t    | 656 ns |      — |\n\nObservations:\n\n- 1t: all three tiers are identical within noise (4-5 ns).\n  The no-wait fast path costs the same at every tier, matching\n  the zc-ring-x1 doc claim that `_with`'s fast path does\n  exactly the loads `reserve_slot` does.\n- 2t: `with` and `spin` are identical (~100 ns) — expected,\n  `reserve_slot_spin` *is* `reserve_slot_with(policy::spin)`.\n  `raw` is consistently ~20 ns slower (119-120 ns) across\n  runs.\n- We think the raw 2t penalty is in the waiting path, not the\n  fast path: the hand-written retry loop re-enters\n  `reserve_slot` on every failed attempt — re-running the type\n  check and re-loading the caller's own index each time — while\n  `_with` hoists the own-index load out of the loop and re-reads\n  only the peer index per attempt, so it reacts faster when the\n  peer's index store lands.\n- Transport context: the zcr 2t rows (~100-120 ns) sit at or\n  under `mpsc-2t-spin` (117 ns) and ~6x under `ice-ps-2t`\n  (656 ns) at the same wait policy.\n\nNet: the ergonomic tiers cost nothing over raw — and under\ncross-core traffic the DIY retry loop is the *slowest* way to\nwait.\n\n# References"
}
```

153	### `all` results (3900X, 0.11.0)
154	
155	One `iiac-perf all` run (default 5 s per bench, unpinned, idle
156	desktop), adjusted mean per bench; probe-based benches report
157	their probes' unadjusted means. Same caveat as above: shapes,
158	not absolutes.
159	
160	| bench             | adjusted mean | note                        |
161	|-------------------|--------------:|-----------------------------|
162	| min-now           |          8 ns | `minstant::Instant::now`    |
163	| std-now           |         21 ns | `std::time::Instant::now`   |
164	| mpsc-1t           |         25 ns | same-thread channel         |
165	| mpsc-2t           |      7,253 ns | blocking `recv` (park/wake) |
166	| mpsc-2t-spin      |        129 ns | spin `try_recv`             |
167	| probe-mpsc-2t     |      7,445 ns | probes: send 744 / 772 ns   |
168	| producer-consumer |             — | probes: 7,511 / 7,532 ns    |
169	| tp-pc             |             — | probes: 7,396 / 7,403 ns    |
170	| tp2-pc            |             — | probes: 7,320 / 7,326 ns    |
171	| ice-ps-1t         |        271 ns | iceoryx2 pub/sub, 1 thread  |
172	| ice-ps-2t         |        699 ns | iceoryx2 pub/sub, 2 threads |
173	| ice-rr-1t         |        800 ns | iceoryx2 req/res, 1 thread  |
174	| ice-rr-2t         |      1,118 ns | iceoryx2 req/res, 2 threads |
175	
176	The wait-policy split dominates the 2-thread rows: the parking
177	benches (`mpsc-2t` and the probe family, all blocking `recv`)
178	cluster at ~7.3-7.5 µs while the spinning benches sit under
179	1.2 µs. For context, iceoryx2's own pub/sub benchmark (v0.9.2,
180	`--bench-all`) on this machine reports 250 ns one-way — ~500 ns
181	round-trip — with pinned realtime threads and untouched payloads,
182	consistent with `ice-ps-2t`'s 699 ns measured here.
183

Its docs claim the no-wait fast path does the same\nloads at every tier; add six benches `zcr-{raw,with,spin}-{1t,2t}`\nto verify the tiers perform basically the same.\n\n### As-built ladder\n\n- 0.13.0-0 `chore: adopt cycle protocol + open zcr cycle`\n- 0.13.0-1 `feat: zcr-raw-1t/2t ring benches`\n- 0.13.0-2 `feat: zcr-with-1t/2t ring benches`\n- 0.13.0-3 `feat: zcr-spin-1t/2t ring benches`\n- 0.13.0-4 `docs: zcr tier comparison results`\n- 0.13.0 `feat: zcr bench family (raw/with/spin, 1t/2t)` —\n  close-out\n\nThe cycle was planned as 0.12.0 but renumbered at `-0`: the\nsession started from a working copy based on 0.11.0, unaware\nthe aarch64 ticks cycle (0.12.0, pushed from another machine)\nalready held `main`.
